28. **B14 `rot_pole_w0` conversion targets an axis 90° from the texture convention — the IAU meridian lands on u 0.75 (mesh x̂) while the shipped convention is u 0.5 (image centre); Iapetus/Amalthea/Proteus render 90° off, the other 17 landed moons carry placeholders. Root: the `+π/2` fudge in `getAxisRotation()` compensates the mesh's −0.25 texcoord, and the conversion reasoned about the MATRIX, inheriting the fudge without its compensation. The gate could not see it — it verifies the same axis the conversion targets.** — ~~**OPEN (found 2026-07-24, §11.101(b); fix belongs in the conversion = ONE authority, code layer, NOT the 20 bodies' data; changes shipped-body output ⇒ gated on A33)**~~ **UNBLOCKED — DECIDED [vixy 2026-07-26, D22 → §11.113(a); A33 CLOSED]: fix in the CONVERSION, ratified.** Derived sub-answers, veto point each: (i) `u = 0.5` (image centre) IS the intended convention — the texture set is NOT re-registered to IAU; (ii) the `+π/2` fudge STAYS at `getAxisRotation()` and the conversion accounts for it (retiring it at the mesh would move EVERY body's meridian, not the three that are wrong); (iii) planet rows untouched. **Gate requirement the answer does not state and this row's own root forces**: the correcting gate must anchor on the TEXTURE (the dark-centroid `u` instrument — `iapetus.png` 0.224 measured vs 0.25 predicted), never on the conversion's own axis; re-running the existing gate after the fix would confirm nothing. Implementation rides **B14**.

29. **Ray-march parent writes PROXY-SHELL depth, not surface depth (`bodyRayMarch.frag` never writes `gl_FragDepth`; the shell is `scaledRadius·(1+0.01·altimetryLevel)`) ⇒ a depth wall of Δ ≈ 34.75 km at b=0 rising to ~349 km at the limb on the Moon, independent of observer distance, over the whole `distance < 64·scaledRadius` band where grounded content is visible. Old-path inherited (`body_moon.cpp:404`), not a new-path regression.** — **FIXED 2026-07-25 (§11.104, code `922701c9`): every fragment now writes the depth of the terrain point it shades, through the vertex stage's own projection expression (binding 0 → VERTEX|FRAGMENT, one authority for the matrix and the bucket range; no regime branch ⇒ the clamp band is covered by construction). MEASURED before/after on the new b3_ladder exact-sphere instrument: the wall inverted from the rendered caps was **34.77 / 34.93 km at two different sites** (= the shell, 34.748 derived from source) and is now **21.33 / 19.74 / 22.78 km — SITE-DEPENDENT**, each within 0.7–2.4 km of that site's own heightmap value (= the terrain). A 30 km grounded sphere at alt 0 goes 0 → 30.3 px cap radius (predicted 28.9–30.1 before the run); a scene with no grounded content is byte-identical pre/post (4 views, 0 px delta). D11 cost NOT material: clock-normalized within-launch ratio 3.8077 → 3.8039, |Δ| ≤ 0.06 ms/frame = 6 % of the 1 ms budget, the null predicted from the mechanism (early-z has nothing to reject in this pass). Old-path baseline unchanged (scene M A/B, same binary, shader swapped: 0 px > 8 on 11239 lit px). NOT covered: §5.30's NIGHT row — whoever closes it must carry the same depth write into `bodyRayMarchNight.frag`, or Earth swaps "no depth" for "shell depth". Found 2026-07-24, §11.101(c); it was the measured-defect justification for B3/D1(b), which now rests on a measured before/after instead.)**

30. **Earth writes NO depth in the ray regime — `LayeredMesh.cpp:229` ORs `VARIANT_NO_DEPTH` for the NIGHT row (old `myEarthShadowed` depth-OFF quirk, parity-inherited) ⇒ depthTest=depthWrite=false within 408,200 km, so grounded occlusion against Earth is impossible by construction at ANY child size.** — **FIXED 2026-07-25 (§11.105, code `d28e67d3`): the OR is retired WITH its requirement named, and the true ray-hit `gl_FragDepth` write lands in `bodyRayMarchNight.frag` in the SAME commit (the §11.104(c) rider). WHY it existed, from the old path's own history: `13d846c8` turned depth off on BOTH ray rows in the same commit that made `body_tes_shadow.vert` emit a CONSTANT `gl_Position.z = 0` (a stage emitting no depth must not write depth — never Earth-specific); `39a8f235` restored depth to the moon-class row only, for `planet_grid`; `b323db09` gave the vertex stage a real projected depth again, retiring the premise for both rows. The Earth row never got either update. MEASURED on the b3_ladder earth altitude ladder, bands committed before each run: RED — a body buried **105 km BELOW the datum renders its whole disc** (all nine legs full, bbox radius 52.0–55.0 px vs 51.8–54.7 predicted); FLIP — the two buried legs go to **0 px** and the shallow ones are cut to the local terrain (n30 32.50 px vs 32.04 predicted; the wall inverted from it is **5.53 km ±1 px 4.85–6.18** against the site's DEM window **5.50–6.01**). The rider's failure mode is EXCLUDED by measurement: the `shell` hypothesis predicts a0 = 0 px and a100 = 42.2 px, measured 53.00 and 53.25. Stock scenes byte-identical pre/post (4 views × 2 module configurations); old path 0 px on 379,637/275,204 lit px; D11 |Δ| ≤ 0.024 ms/frame = 2.4 % of budget. NOT sufficient on its own — see §5.33.)**

31. **Hidden bodies tick and DRAW their orbit lines — the orbit LINE sweep (`ModularSystem.cpp:511-530`) lacks the `if (!body) continue;` gate its TRACE sibling has (`:492`), and `hide()` never deregisters from `sortedSystemBodies` (`removeBody` is destruction-only). B19's own tick opened the channel by making `distance` non-zero (corrections-are-error-candidates). The TRAIL half was later ratified deliberately (§11.56); the ORBIT half never was.** — ~~**OPEN (found 2026-07-24, §11.101(e); §11.54(i)'s boundary claim was false at source and false when written; old-path comparison is the discriminator per §11.52(b) ⇒ A34)**~~ **ANSWERED AND WIDENED [vixy 2026-07-26, D23 → §11.113(b); A34 CLOSED] — the row's question was one instance of a semantic the answer settles engine-wide**: *"hidden body should behave as if they didn't exist while hidden, and behave as if they never were hidden when unhidden. For performance reason, hidden bodies shouldn't tick. Hiding a body implicitly hide his child body as a side effect of nesting, but mustn't change the exposed hidden attribute/flag."* ⇒ (i) the orbit line is a DEFECT, gate authorised; (ii) EVERY annotation of a hidden body leaves the frame (orbit, trail DRAW, hints/labels, axis, grid, pointer, shadows, occlusion, click-pick) — **this supersedes the HIDDEN half of §11.56**, whose tester-ratified rule governs the display-FLAG gate and is untouched there; (iii) B19's tick retires for hidden bodies into the §11.76 use-site barrier (the mechanism B32/§11.93 already proved; `b19_hidden_tick.py` reads through a use channel ⇒ stays green and discriminating, re-verify rather than inherit); (iv) unhide IS a use — computable state reconstructs, accumulated HISTORY (trail points) only where the past is computable; the impossible case degrades and LOGS (D12); (v) implicit child hiding is already satisfied by the new path's parking design (`ModularBody.cpp:445-481`) and VIOLATED by the old path (→ new §5.44). **Boundary, from the corpus not from argument [observed 2026-07-29]**: `S10.sts:17-18` uses a `hidden = true` body as home_planet AND selects it; `W17.sts:14-18` toggles one live ⇒ hidden removes the body from the RENDERED universe only, never from the command/structural surface (name lookup, reference/anchor role, selection-by-name), and *being the reference is itself a use*. Implementation → **B39**. — **CLOSED 2026-07-30 by B39 (§11.117): the root the row itself named is fixed (`hide()` now takes the parked subtree OUT of `sortedSystemBodies`, so the line sweep — and nine other contributions — never reach a hidden body), measured on this row's own discriminator: hidden orbit line 779 px>32 → 0, against a shown-body control that stays 779 px. NB the row's suggested shape ("the sweep gains the visibility test its TRACE sibling has") was deliberately NOT taken: that test is `operator bool` = on-screen AND not hidden, and adding it would also delete the orbit line of every body outside the view cone — a semantics change D23 does not ask for (§11.117(a)).**

32. **Camera-reference cached state goes stale — the reference is the ONE node that can skip `update()` (`ModularBody.cpp:247-261`, sole writer of `axisRotation`) while its consumers read it with no drawn gate (`Camera.cpp:92,476,591-599,510,909`). `Camera.hpp:425`'s `boundToSurface = true` is a persistent default, not the altitude invariant §11.93(b)'s dismissal assumed. Sharpest: `Camera.cpp:591/599` writes stale spin into PERSISTENT observer longitude (does not round-trip). Same node latches `areaOfInfluence` ⇒ AoI is less fresh than the `eclipticPos` it derives from.** — **OPEN (found 2026-07-24, §11.101(d); one fix — recompute the reference's spin+reach at the top of `Camera::update` — closes spin, longitude and the reference half of the B15 residual; existing gates blind by construction)** **[ANNOTATED 2026-07-25, F4 §11.108(e) — FIRST MEASURED OBSERVABLE OF THIS CLASS, and it adds a consumer the row did not list.** `Camera::descend` (`Camera.cpp:836-877`) reads the reference through `getObservedPosition()` = `mat.getTranslation()` (`ModularBody.hpp:1075-1077`), the cached eye-frame matrix refreshed only by the per-frame body update ⇒ **N uses inside ONE frame all see the same geometry and compound LINEARLY**: ten `descend(0.99)` with no intervening frame gave alt ×**0.899997** (measured 179.999429 km) against the geometric ×**0.9043821** (measured 180.876177 km with frames between); the linear prediction from the mechanism is ×0.9 exactly = 180.000019 km, so the residual is **0.59 m**. User-reachable: a script or the TCP channel can issue several `camera action descend` in one frame — a **D8 use-site-barrier** breach, not just a staleness nit. Same fix as the row's (recompute the reference's state at the top of `Camera::update`).]**

33. **The ATMOSPHERE shell writes depth it does not own — `AtmExtModule` is `BMT_TRANSLUCENT` (⇒ drawn LAST in the parent's near list) with depth test AND WRITE on, at `atmosphere_radius_factor · scaledRadius`, so its surface is the LAST thing in the parent's merged depth bucket: a second wall, taller than the proxy shell §5.29 removed and than any terrain §5.30 exposed (1.03 vs 1.02 vs 1.02·h on Earth). Grounded occlusion against stock Earth stayed impossible after §5.30. The write was never a requirement: `atm_ext.cpp:16` never calls `setDepthStencilMode`, so depth-write ON is the EntityCore ctor default, inherited; the module header always stated the contract as "depth-tested".** — **FOUND AND FIXED 2026-07-25 (§11.105, code `ded43e5a`; forced scope expansion of the §5.30 task, recorded as such): `color.state.depthWrite = false`, test stays ON. MEASURED: the wall inverted from the one metering leg is **191.47 km (±1 px 190.59–192.31)** against **191.3442 km** derived from `atmosphere_radius_factor = 1.03` — 0.067 %, and not the shell (127.56) nor the terrain (5.5); ATTRIBUTED by counterfactual (the same code and ladder with `[Earth:ATMOSPHERE]` dropped from the composed twin has no wall at all). After the fix stock Earth is byte-identical, leg by leg, to that counterfactual. No collateral change: content-free stock scenes byte-identical on all four views including the fov-150 one where the rim IS present (it differs by md5 from the ATMOSPHERE-dropped scene, so the compared surface provably carries the content). Reaches every body with an `atmosphere_ext_model` (Earth/Mars/Venus/Titan); RING is the other `BMT_TRANSLUCENT` module and is NOT of this class (a ring is a real occluder — no blanket rule).**

34. **`Object::operator=` never releases the previous rep — both overloads (`tools/object.cpp:105-126`) overwrite `rep` after retaining the new one, so every reassignment of an `Object` leaks one reference.** Pre-existing and already live on the OLD path: a STAR selection leaks its refcounted `StarWrapperBase` on every subsequent selection change (the wrapper self-deletes at zero and never reaches zero). B24-select adds a second instance of the class — each composed-body selection leaks one `ModularObject` (~24 B) **plus a permanent entry in the static `ModularBodyPtr::ref` vector**, which is scanned on every body destruction. Bodies whose `retain`/`release` are no-ops (old `Body`, owned by `ProtoSystem`) are unaffected, which is why the defect stayed invisible. — **OPEN (recorded 2026-07-25, §11.106(h); NOT fixed with the selection wave: releasing correctly changes object lifetime on the old path — a star wrapper that today survives by leaking would be destroyed — so it needs its own enumeration of who holds raw `ObjectBase*` and its own verification)**

35. **`day_key_mode` is an INERT capability end to end — a control surface that changes nothing.** `App::setDayKeyMode` (`app.hpp:129-130`) declares `std::string DayKeyMode = _value;` — a **local that shadows the member** — so the setter is a no-op; its only caller is the TUI's "Day keys" item (`ui_tuiconf.cpp:1036`, calendar/sidereal). And the member itself has **no behavioural consumer anywhere**: read from config (`app.cpp:628`), displayed in the TUI (`ui_tuiconf.cpp:688`), written back on save (`app.cpp:931`) — while the day keys emit `date sidereal ±1` / `date relative ±1` as hardcoded strings (`ui.cpp:1506,1523,1689,1705,1716,1731,1857,1874`). So the operator can select a mode that neither sticks nor would do anything if it did. — **OPEN (found by the S6 capability audit 2026-07-25, §11.108(f); NOT fixed: un-shadowing alone makes an inert value stick and get persisted to config, and giving it a consumer changes what the day keys DO — a user-visible semantics decision, not an implementation step)**

36. **`media subtitle toggle` turns subtitles OFF instead of toggling — a wrong-variable test, one word from correct.** `app_command_interface.cpp:3329` tests `argAction == W_TOGGLE` inside the branch guarded by `!argSubtitle.empty()`; the same command family's other implementation, 150 lines above at `:3178`, tests `argSubtitle == W_TOGGLE` and is right. So `media subtitle toggle` leaves `argAction` empty, falls through to `Utility::isTrue("toggle") == false` and calls `playerSetShowSubtitles(false)`. — **OPEN (found by the F4 command-grammar census 2026-07-25, §11.108(k) / B38; NOT fixed although the fix is one word: its counterfactual is not observable on this host — `~/.spacecrafter/videos` is empty and the subtitle state has no readout on any control channel — and an unverified user-visible behaviour change is exactly what the S-class rule excludes. Lands trivially in any wave that carries a media asset; the sibling at `:3178` is the specification.)**

37. **The whole galactic/addSystem surface is DEAD — `loadGalacticSystem` opens `.galactic.ini`, and `addSystem` opens `.stellar_systems/<file>`.** `core.cpp:320` calls `loadGalacticSystem(".", "galactic.ini")` and the callee opens `path + name` with no separator (`ssystem_factory.cpp:470`); the same missing separator repeats one level down at `:503` (`path + params["system"]`). So on a shipped install **no galactic entry is read, no foreign star system is created, no galactic anchor is added, and no galactic twin is ever generated** — the fallback `addAnchor("Sun", …)` branch is the only thing that runs. Introduced by `da858612c` (2025-09-20, *"Merge work between body-refactoring and 2023-master"*), which replaced `AppSettings::Instance()->getUserDir()` (a path WITH its trailing `/`) by `"."` across a batch where every other call simply dropped the prefix — `loadGalacticSystem` was the one two-argument call and needed `"./"`. MEASURED, not read (§11.109(a)): shipped tree → 0 `Params :` blocks, 0 galactic system nodes, 0 galactic twins; a byte-identical copy of the same file under the dotted name → 18 blocks, 17 nodes; adding the per-system files under the dotted directory → 17 twins. Zero of 66 recorded applogs contains a `Params :` block. — ~~**OPEN, FIX SUSPENDED (found 2026-07-25, §11.109(a)): … ⇒ DECISIONS_PENDING D29**~~ **OPEN, FIX AUTHORISED [vixy 2026-07-26, D29 → §11.113(h); A39 CLOSED]: option (1) — repair path AND parse in ONE commit, the parse first inside it.** The 17 systems light up (empty on this install; a delivery with content arrives populated), and the answer adds two REQUIREMENTS beyond the option: the parser must accept arbitrary whitespace around `=`, and it must support COMMENTS. NOT adopted [derived, veto]: the supervising session's optional empty-system anchor suppression — plain (1) stands, the ghost anchors are accepted as benign and are what makes the 0-byte corpora noticeable. §5.38 still rides on it and is fixed WITH it, never after. Implementation → **B40**. — **FIXED 2026-07-30 (§11.115, code `2e54be6f`, ONE commit with §5.38/§5.39): `loadGalacticSystem` owns the directory/file join once (the separator belongs to the join, not to the caller), so `core.cpp`'s `"."` resolves to `./galactic.ini` and `./stellar_systems/<file>` again. MEASURED on the real install, binary the only variable: `Params :` blocks **0 → 18**, galactic system nodes **0 → 17**, twins **1 → 18**, 103 pre-existing bodies with **0 divergent fields**; frozen data md5 in == out. The 17 systems arrive EMPTY on this install (13 zero-byte corpora) and their twins are banner-only, as predicted.**

38. **`galactic.ini`'s key/value parse corrupts seven shipped coordinates.** `ssystem_factory.cpp:481` does `params[line.substr(0,pos-1)] = line.substr(pos+2)` — it assumes exactly `key = value` with one space on each side of `=`. On the installed `~/.spacecrafter/galactic.ini`, 15 lines diverge from a correct parse and **7 of them change the number**: five lose a minus sign (`Keid z`, `HelixDwarf y`, `M57Dwarf y`, `M27Dwarf y`, `NGC2392Dwarf x` — written `z =-2.371937` style, no space after `=`) and two lose a LEADING DIGIT (`M1Pulsar y = 1988.889006` → `988.889006`, `NGC2392Dwarf y = 1858.478137` → `858.478137`). Six of the seventeen systems would sit at wrong galactic coordinates. — **OPEN, FIX AUTHORISED [vixy 2026-07-26, D29 → §11.113(h)] (found 2026-07-25, §11.109(i)): unreachable today because §5.37 keeps the file from being opened at all, which is exactly why the two must be fixed together — repairing the path alone would place six systems wrong on first light. The parse is the same class as §5.39 and the same one-line shape as `ModularSystem::loadSystem`'s** — and the answer makes that unification a requirement, not an option: *"improving the parser (to handle space before/after the =) and supporting comments are both required as well"*. Blast radius of hardening on the SHIPPED corpus is already measured at §5.39 (six inert + one dropped-either-way) ⇒ zero behavioural change there; a field file with `radius  = 100` starts working, which is the author's intent. Re-measure those seven before/after rather than inheriting the claim. → **B40**. — **FIXED 2026-07-30 (§11.115(c), code `2e54be6f`, same commit as §5.37, parse first inside it): the seven were re-measured, not inherited, and the fix was shown able to FAIL — a staging binary with the path repaired and this parse left alone put **6 of 17 systems at wrong coordinates** (`HelixDwarfSystem` +100.291 for −100.291, `M1PulsarSystem` 988.889 for 1988.889, `NGC2392DwarfSystem` (704.43, 858.48) for (−704.43, 1858.48), …), against **0 of 17** with the full fix, every node checked against `galactic.ini` read correctly.**

39. **Two parsers read one legacy star-system format — the loader's and the twin generator's.** `ModularSystem::loadSystem` (`ModularSystem.cpp:1349-1381`) splits lines with its own `substr` arithmetic; `generateComposedTwin` re-reads the SAME file through `ModularSystemFormat::parse` (`ModularSystemFormat.cpp:23-58`), which trims properly and skips lines without `=`. Where they disagree the twin is not a faithful record of what was loaded (I2: two authorities for one format). MEASURED on the shipped `ssystem.ini`: **7 keys diverge** — six whitespace-only and inert under `strtod` (`[saturn]`/`[Goldilocks_Zone] ring_inner_size`, `[jupiter] rot_rotation_offset`, `[prospero]`/`[setebos] orbit_ascendingnode`, `[sinope] color`) and one structural, `[Sedna]`'s malformed `orbit_LongOfPericenter 95.58754` (no `=`), which the loader turns into a garbage key and the format parser drops. Hand-authored paid data need not be so lucky: `radius  = 100` (two spaces) is read by the loader as key `"radius "` — the body gets NO radius — while the twin declares `radius = 100`. — **OPEN, PARTIALLY SUSPENDED (found 2026-07-25, §11.109(i)/(h2)): unifying the parse (one authority, the loader's rules, used by both) is decision-free and closes the desync, but NOT the residual — `ModularSystemFormat::write` emits `key = value` and `parse` trims, so a key the loader read with whitespace cannot be represented in the composed format at all. That last step is a format-semantics decision (quote? tighten the legacy parser on frozen data?) ⇒ suspended.** **[UNSUSPENDED 2026-07-26 by two answers → §11.113(h)(n): D29 makes the hardened, whitespace-tolerant parse a REQUIREMENT (so the divergence closes at its root — a trimmed key is representable), and D35 makes legacy READ-ONLY forever (so byte-faithful legacy round-trip is no longer a requirement at all; what remains is the twin's fidelity as a RECORD). Neither the quoting option nor the frozen-data-tightening dilemma survives: nothing rewrites legacy, and the tightening's blast radius on shipped data is measured zero (six inert + `[Sedna]` dropped either way). Implementation rides B40 with §5.37/§5.38 — one parse authority, the loader's rules, used by both.]** — **FIXED 2026-07-30 (§11.115(b)(d), code `2e54be6f`): the authority is `src/tools/ini_line.hpp`, used by FOUR readers (this loader, `ModularSystemFormat::parse`, the galactic reader, and `CameraAnchors::load`, which had acquired a fourth copy of the same arithmetic in §11.111). The OLD path's `ProtoSystem::load` is deliberately NOT joined — frozen baseline (§11.52(b)) — and now carries the reason at the site. Re-measured on the shipped `ssystem.ini`: **nine** keys parse differently (the six whitespace cases, `[Sedna]`'s `=`-less line now NAMED by a §2(f) warning instead of becoming a garbage key, and two trailing comments the comment requirement adds), **leading number changed on zero of the nine**; parse-only staging binary vs pre-fix binary = 103 bodies, **0 divergent fields**. The representability residual DISSOLVES as §11.113(n) predicted: the loader no longer reads a key with whitespace in it, so every key it reads is expressible as `key = value`.**

40. **The composed twin declared bodies belonging to OTHER systems** — `generateComposedTwin` resolved every legacy section through `ModularBody::findBodyOnce` (`ModularSystem.cpp:1496`), the GLOBAL name→body registry, which answers *"does this name exist anywhere"* and not *"did THIS system load it"*; `loadBody` skips a section whose name is already taken (`:963`), so for a name another system loaded first the lookup returned a FOREIGN body and its live capabilities were emitted into this system's twin. Reachable on the SHIPPED `galactic.ini`, which points five entries at one `stellar_systems` file. — **FIXED 2026-07-25 (§11.109(c), code `14bb627f`): membership is answered by the TREE (`ModularBody::isInSubtreeOf`, I4), never by the name registry; the omitted section gets a §2(f)/D12 warning naming both fixes. MEASURED pre-fix: 5 byte-identical twins (`3392f303`) for 1 real + 4 EMPTY systems; post-fix: owner unchanged, 4 banner-only files (`719ab4c5`), 8 warnings. Solar twin byte-identical across the fix (`63dd3004`).**

41. **`camera action save` cannot succeed as shipped (doubled path prefix), and destroys the date when it can.** `CoreLink::cameraSave` prepends `"anchors/"` (`coreLink.cpp:57-59`) and `SSystemFactory::cameraSave` prepends `"anchors/"` **again** (`ssystem_factory.hpp:871-873`), so `AnchorManager::saveCameraPosition` (`anchor_manager.cpp:696-739`) opens `anchors/anchors/<name>`; the load leg doubles identically (`coreLink.cpp:62-64`, `ssystem_factory.hpp:875-877`). `~/.spacecrafter/anchors` does not exist on this install ⇒ the command always fails. **MEASURED, ladder one variable at a time, predictions first** (2026-07-25, §11.110(d)): no `anchors/` → FAILS with `AnchorManager::saveCameraPosition couldn't create a file anchors/anchors/f6test`; `anchors/` created → **still FAILS, same path**; `anchors/anchors/` created → **succeeds**. Two further properties of the file it then writes: it carries **old-path authorities only** (Observer lat/lon/alt, `Navigator::getLocalVision`, TimeMgr jd — no heading, fov, mount, reference or selection), and it is written with **default ostream precision**, so the session's JD **2461247.34443** came back as `time 2.46125e+06` = **2461250** — a **2.66-day error** with a **10-day** round-trip resolution. — **OPEN (found by the B31 design pass 2026-07-25, §11.110(d); NOT fixed: the prefix is a one-line fix but the surface is a user-visible command whose repair makes a previously-dead path live, and the precision half changes the file's format; both belong in a wave that can verify them. B31's design (§3.4(d)) requires this surface to be re-expressed on the session serializer rather than extended.)**

42. **`configuration action save` rewrites the user's `config.ini` destroying every comment, and `InitParser::load` aborts the process on one malformed line.** `App::saveCurrentConfig` (`app.cpp:907-941`) → `InitParser::save` (`init_parser.cpp:75-86`) → `iniparser_dump_ini` (`iniparser.c:258-281`), which re-emits the parsed dictionary as `%-30s = %s` and has no representation for comments. **MEASURED on a farm copy** (2026-07-25, §11.110(c), 8 predictions committed first, all matched): comment lines **3 → 0** (file-level AND in-section); an inline `; …` trailing comment stripped out of its value; `F6_MixedCase_Key` → **`f6_mixedcase_key`** (`strlwc`, `iniparser.c:657-688`); unknown key and unknown section preserved; key/section order preserved; and **9 keys the user never authored materialized** (`nautical_alt`, `nautical_ra`, `object_coordinates`, `mouse_coordinates`, `angular_distance`, `loxodromy`, `orthodromy`, `vertical_line`, `flag_star_pick`), 325 → 330 lines. Two source-certain riders: the save opens the **target itself** with `fopen(…, "wt")` (`init_parser.cpp:79`) — truncate in place, no sibling temp, no rename ⇒ **non-atomic**, the failure mode §11.52(a) clause 2 exists to prevent; and `iniparser_load` returns NULL if ANY line is a syntax error (`iniparser.c:812-818`), whereupon `InitParser::load` calls **`exit(-1)`** (`:62-72`) ⇒ **a single malformed line in `config.ini` kills the app at startup**. So one user-editable file is governed by refusal-on-read and destruction-on-write — the exact inverse of the §11.66(b) preservation contract mandated for the other user-editable format. — **OPEN (found by the B31 design pass 2026-07-25, §11.110(c); NOT fixed: making the config writer comment-preserving is the §11.66(b) writer's job on a second format, and making the reader survive a bad line changes startup semantics on frozen field configs — both are B31-adjacent work with their own verification. The immediate design consequence is recorded instead: the session file is NOT `config.ini`, and B31 must not extend this writer's reach.)**

43. **`ModularBodyPtr`'s implicitly generated copy constructor produced an UNREGISTERED pointer — its destructor then walked the tracking list off the end.** The class registers each instance in the static `ref` vector and increments the body's `pointerCount` in its raw-pointer constructor; the implicit COPY constructor did neither, while the destructor unconditionally runs `while (ref[++i] != this);` [observed: `ModularBodyPtr.cpp:14-24`] — so destroying a copy scanned past the end of `ref` (SIGSEGV), and while the copy lived the body's pin count was one short of its actual holders (the very I5 guarantee this pointer exists to provide). Latent as long as every ModularBodyPtr lived at a stable address; **MEASURED the first time one lived inside a value a container RELOCATES** (2026-07-25, the B4 anchor registry): SIGSEGV at `ModularBodyPtr.cpp:19` inside `__relocate_object_a`, on the 6th anchor of the shipped `anchor.ini`, first run (backtrace in §11.111(f)). — **FIXED 2026-07-25 (§11.111(f), code `2d1387b5`): the copy constructor delegates to the raw-pointer constructor, so registration keeps ONE authority (I2) and a copy is exactly a second holder.** Rider recorded, NOT fixed: `ModularBodySelector` inherits that constructor and its own destructor deselects, so copying a SELECTOR remains a hazard of a different kind (no crash — a spurious deselect when the copy dies); nothing copies one today.

44. **OLD PATH: unhiding a body RE-SHOWS descendants that were hidden independently — `showBodyRecursive` has no memory of why a child was hidden.** `ProtoSystem::setPlanetHidden` routes both directions through the recursive pair (`protosystem.cpp:271-298`): `hideBodyRecursive` hides every descendant, `showBodyRecursive` **shows** every descendant unconditionally — while `hideBody`/`showBody` are pure membership edits on `renderedBodies`/`sortedRenderedBodies` (`protosystem.hpp:196-203`) that record nothing about the origin of the state they change. So a body authored `hidden = true` (`initialHidden`, `:947-966`) or hidden by a script, sitting under a parent the operator hides and then unhides, comes back VISIBLE — the authored/commanded state is destroyed by an unrelated operation on an ancestor. Reachable from the shipped command surface (`body name <parent> hidden true|false`); the shipped `[Solar_System]`/`[Goldilocks_Zone]` hidden bodies hang off `Sun`, so `body name Sun hidden true` then `false` is the two-command reproduction. — **OPEN, OLD PATH ONLY, NOT TO BE FIXED THERE (found 2026-07-29 by the D23 propagation's corpus check, §11.113(b)(v); the old path is the frozen comparison baseline and this is a defect in it, tracked not reproduced per §11.52(b)).** The NEW path is already correct **by construction, not by a guard**: `hide()` parks the node in its parent's `hiddenBodies` with ownership and relation intact (`ModularBody.cpp:445-481`), so the subtree leaves every walk as a side effect of nesting and no descendant's own state is touched — which is exactly what [vixy, D23] requires (*"mustn't change the exposed hidden attribute/flag"*). The row exists so B39 does not "port" the old behaviour on the assumption that old is the spec, and so the divergence is named before it is measured.

45. **A `galactic.ini` section missing `x`, `y` or `z` KILLS THE APP AT STARTUP — and it is §5.37's repair that makes it reachable.** `SSystemFactory::loadSystem` does `systemOffsets[params["name"]].set(stod(params["x"]), stod(params["y"]), stod(params["z"]))` [observed: `ssystem_factory.cpp`, the line after `addAnchor`] on a `stringHash_t` whose `operator[]` returns `""` for an absent key; `std::stod("")` throws `std::invalid_argument`, and neither `main.cpp` nor `core.cpp` has a `catch` [observed: zero `catch` in either]. **MEASURED 2026-07-30 (§11.115(i))** on a farm copy with ONE `z` line removed and nothing else changed: the process dies before opening its port, `returncode -6` (SIGABRT), `terminate called after throwing an instance of 'std::invalid_argument' / what(): stod` — while the anchor layer, called one line earlier on the SAME params, had already declined the section gracefully (`AnchorPointCreator::handle x y or z parameter missing`, `error addAnchor AnchorPoint nullptr Proxima`). The shipped corpus is well-formed (18 sections, all three keys each), so nothing is broken today; a paid galactic delivery with one malformed section refuses to start the product. — ~~**OPEN (found 2026-07-30 by B40, §11.115(i); NOT fixed there: the task's boundary is record-don't-fix, and "skip vs refuse" on paid data is a user-visible semantic. The shape is barely a choice, though — the same function's own anchor call already decides to decline and log, so matching it is consistency (I2/§2(f)), not invention: guard `name`/`x`/`y`/`z`, warn naming the section and the missing key, skip. Recommended as the first item of the next galactic dispatch.)~~ **FIXED 2026-07-30 (F12 item iv, code `41f68121`, §11.118(a))**: `name`/`x`/`y`/`z` are guarded at the top of `loadSystem`, the section is NAMED by its `[header]` (carried in, because it is the only identifier left when `name` is the missing key), the diagnostic states the remedy (§2(f)) and the skip is LOGGED (D12). Guarded on PARSEABILITY, not presence — `x = ,5` and `x = 1e999` throw from the same line — while any value `stod` PARSES is accepted exactly as before, partial parses included, so no data that loads today changes meaning. Measured, `harness/f12_s545.py`, 8 launches: `[Proxima] z` deleted and `[Keid] x = ,5` give rc **−6** with no port on the pre-fix binary and warn+skip+start on the patched one, every other system still loading (18 == 19 − 1). **The record was incomplete**: a missing `name` never aborted — `params["name"]` yields `""` and the old code built a system node literally called `System`, losing `TolimanSystem` silently [measured]. Byte-inert on the shipped corpus: `b40_parity` pre vs post = 120 bodies, 0 divergent fields, 18 twins identical.**

46. **`dispatchUpdate`'s UP-CHAIN never writes `matLocalToBodyPos`, though the member's own contract says it is *"Set on EVERY position update (visible or not)"*.** [observed 2026-07-30, §11.117(k)(2): the `while (body->isNotIsolated)` loop assigns `body->mat = parentTilted` and calls `preUpdate`/`update`, and nothing writes the flat position frame; every DOWNWARD path does write it — `recursiveUpdate`, `selectiveUpdate`'s else-branch and `recursiveTranslationUpdate` all set it explicitly and say why.] So for every ancestor ABOVE the camera's reference body the cached flat frame is whatever it was when the walk last descended through it. Consequence beyond the finding: `ModularSystem::drawOrbits`/`drawTrails`/`drawTails` build each body's parent frame from `body.getMatLocalToBodyPos()`, so an up-chain ancestor's OWN orbit/trail/tail line is placed in a stale frame — reachable on today's corpus (observer on the Moon ⇒ Earth is up-chain and carries a TRAIL module), which is exactly the class §11.39 believed it had closed (*"the cached frame is correct for EVERY body, visible or not"* — true of the descent, false of the climb). — **OPEN (found 2026-07-30 by B39; NOT fixed there: the one-line fix CHANGES RENDERED OUTPUT for a body B39 does not otherwise touch, and B39 does not need it — `publishParkedFrame` captures the frame at the six sites that provably have it. Fix shape: assign `matLocalToBodyPos = flat` in the up-chain loop where `mat = parentTilted` is assigned, and A/B the orbit/trail of an up-chain ancestor to see what moves.)**


47. **`get status position` produces NO REPLY on the client connection.** [observed+measured 2026-07-30, §11.118(i)] `AppCommandInterface::commandGet` resolves the `status position` argument and calls `tcp->setOutput(coreLink->tcpGetPosition())`; `ServerSocket::setOutput` pushes the string onto `outputQueue` under its lock and returns. Nothing arrived on the driving socket in a **6 s poll, twice**, in a session where `timerate`, `date`, `flag`, `select`, `body action dual_dump` and `body action screenshot` all worked on that same connection — the app's own log carries no "No tcp : i can't send" warning either, so the command ran and the queue was fed. Consequence beyond the missing string: `get status position` is the ONLY side-effect-free read of the observer position AND of the heading readout, so its silence forces every heading instrument through `heading delta_azimuth`, which WRITES both authorities after it reads (that is why §11.118(f)'s two `experimental_path` pins needed two launches). — **OPEN (found 2026-07-30 by F12/B33; not chased: the delivery had a working, if write-after-read, channel and the drain path is a TCP-server question, not a body-path one. Fix shape: follow `outputQueue` through `ServerSocket::run`'s send loop and find out whether it is drained at all, drained to a different socket, or drained only when a second client is connected.)**

48. **The §11.18 `ASmooth` NaN still fires on a cold `$HOME`, on whichever body the config scales at init.** [measured 2026-07-30, §11.118(i)] On a freshly built temp-HOME farm the app intermittently leaves `ModularBody::scaling` non-finite, so `boundingRadius`, `scaledDatumRadius` and `scaledGroundRadius` all dump as `nan` — observed on the **Moon** once and on the **Sun** once, which is exactly the pair `SSystemFactory::setPlanetSizeScale` targets by name from `moon_scale`/`sun_scale` at startup. `EntityCore/Executor/ASmooth.hpp` already carries a guard for the same shape, with the same observable in its own comment (*"double setScaling at init → Moon scaledRadius NaN → frozen main loop once the Moon became the camera reference"*), so this is a residue of that class rather than a new one. **Attributed to NEITHER binary**: 2 fires in the first 6 cold launches across two binaries, then **0 of 4 vs 0 of 4** in a controlled alternating repeat of the same corpus. — **OPEN (found 2026-07-30 by F12; NOT fixed: `EntityCore/` is read-only for this work, and the trigger is a startup race whose reproduction rate is ~15 % at best. Instrument consequence CLOSED at the root in the same wave: `b24_equivalence.load_dump` used to drop a NaN-carrying body silently — C++ prints `nan`, Python's json rejects it — so the body read as ABSENT to every gate built on that loader; it is now parsed and visible, and fails a value check loudly instead of disappearing.)**

## 6. Decisions — open and resolved (resolved entries keep their answer)

1. **Priority ladder naming** — **RESOLVED** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: `ResourcePriority` naming wins; legacy `NOW` renamed to `ACTIVE`; strategy.txt updated (verified in tree).
2. **Inline-in-header policy** — **OPEN, context added**: headers are the thinking medium [vixy: 2026-07-11], so implementation-in-header is partly drafting state; G10 justifies keeping *hot* paths inline. Still to decide: which inlines are load-bearing (stay) vs drafting residue (migrate to .cpp once parity is proven).
3. **`BodyType` residual role** — **DERIVED (2026-07-19, per §2(a2)'s gates-vs-parameters test — recommendation recorded, enum surgery awaits Vixy since the enum is header vocabulary)**: `MINOR_BODY` justified [vixy: 2026-07-11] AND consumer-confirmed — 3 capability sites (shadow exemption, ModularSystem.cpp:154/208/237). **SPHERICAL_BODY and SINGLE_BODY have ZERO consumers and ZERO producers** [measured: grep 2026-07-19 — `strToBodyType` never assigns them (Asteroid/KBO/Comet→MINOR, all named types→CUSTOM_BODY, ModularSystem.cpp:40-55); no site tests them]. Under §2(a2): SPHERICAL's "doesn't use self-shadowing" duplicates what module traits already express (BMT_* absence is the authority — I2 violation as a second home); SINGLE/CUSTOM's "single vs multiple shapes" duplicates the components list itself. All three are identity declarations, not capabilities → **recommendation: collapse to ONE default value** (keep the `CUSTOM_BODY` spelling or rename `DEFAULT`); the oblateness-fast-path speculation found no code basis (oblateness rides `getOneMinusOblateness()` per body, type-untested).
4. **Taskable / cross-thread handoff** — **ARCHITECTURE CONVERGED [vixy: 2026-07-17]**; §8's v2 shape confirmed at its load-bearing point. Vixy's statement, verbatim: *"the single chain is my intent for the finaliser/integration part, that is integrating the fully-processed part into the scene so that it can be rendered without race."* What this confirms, precisely (do not over-read it): (i) **§8.2.5's single-Taskable granularity is INTENT, not a chosen tradeoff** — so its consequences stand on intent now: non-atomic pins, the forced hand-off rule, application-lifetime Taskables, `TASKABLE_REFERENCE_COUNT`/`KEEP_REFERENCE` off; (ii) **§8.2.3's two protection domains are confirmed by the words "fully-processed"** — what reaches the chain is already finished, so §8.4's precondition 2 (integration tasks are O(small)) is *definitional*, not merely hoped: a chain task that does hard work violates the intent, not just a budget; (iii) the chain's role is **finalisation/integration into the scene, race-free** — i.e. C1 is discharged by chain serialisation as §8.2.1 states. **Still open (implementation, not architecture)**: §8.5's parameters (pool sizing, VRAM ladder + degradation values, preservationCycles, attachment-list mechanism, s_texture/registry dedup overlap, the Task memory-validity flag candidate); §8.2.10's screen-capture/video-render integration item (flagged, never designed); §8.6(f)'s KEEP_REFERENCE refcount underflow — **stays MOOT under single-chain (now intent-backed) but remains latent**, with §8.2.5's reopening trigger (sustained publish volume contending with frame-task latency → per-body split → the flag returns → (f) must be fixed first). **S4 is architecturally unblocked**; the frame-task-rides-the-same-chain half was already [vixy: 2026-07-11].
5. **EnvironmentModule scope** — **RESOLVED** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: everything camera-location-relative "outside" — milkyway 2D(earth-centered)/3D, atmosphere, landscape, etc. (§3.8). ~~Open remainder: the module's interface~~ implemented 2026-07-16 (§11.27).
6. **Milkyway root lifetime** — **RESOLVED (2026-07-17, §11.36)**: the universe root exists (`unique_ptr<ModularSystem> universe`, factory-owned, declared before the camera for destruction order); every node now has a parent for remnant-ModularBodyPtr delegation and the **universe is the single eternal node** (redirect-to-nullptr only at final teardown — ModularBodyPtr::redirect null-guarded for exactly that). The milkyway is an ordinary INNER child of the universe; MilkyWayEnv rides it unchanged.

8. **Orientation convention (P5) — DIRECTION RESOLVED, implementation open** [vixy 2026-07-17]: user scripts cannot be modified, so the missing orientation class is to be **managed in the least intrusive way in the new path** — NOT by re-authoring data (ruled out by construction: scripts declare `rot_obliquity` through `body action load`, §11.34). Root cause derived in **§11.34** (role conflation in old's `rot_local_to_parent` + a `while`→`if` truncation that kept the rotation half of a chain whose translation half was correctly flattened; shipped data is authored under the resulting nested-frame convention). Divergence set closed at 7 bodies (Sun, Moon, Charon, Nix, Hydra_, Styx, Kerberos). **DECISION [vixy 2026-07-17, on the confirmed structural check]: route the render tilt through the `accumulatedBodyPosToBody` frame the new path already uses for observer semantics** — I2, one authority for the body's equatorial frame, one site.
   **Premise (the thing that makes it a fix and not a match)**: the body's lat/lon grid is ONE frame with TWO consumers — the camera/observer declaration frame (ModularBody.cpp:168, accumulated) and the mesh render frame (ModularBody.cpp:150, own-tilt-only) — and they must be identical because the mesh is lat/lon-textured and the observer stands on it. The header already states this [ModularBody.hpp:397-399] and the render path contradicts it. Verified: the two frames differ by **23.4421°** on the Moon today; the fix closes them to **0.0000°** algebraically (`mat = C·A⁻¹·A = C`). This is an internal contradiction of the new path, independent of the old path — which is why the fix generalizes implicitly (all ancestors not one; correct nesting order; Sun guard already inside the walk; script-declared bodies covered structurally) instead of reproducing old's truncation.
   **Precondition (1) — precession: RESOLVED → (A)** [vixy: 2026-07-17]: **accumulated + precessed at both consumers.** Old's own observer and render disagree by **0.3631° (2026)**, so there was no self-consistent old behaviour to reproduce; (A) matches old's *observer* exactly (0.0000°) and self-consistently, residual vs old's *render* = Earth's precession only. **Reason given, and it is the general criterion, not a one-off** [vixy]: *"the physical truth is the main priority of spacecrafter, because it's for educative purpose"* → §2 lead. Under that criterion the choice is not close: (A) is both self-consistent AND nearer physical truth; the 0.363° that (B) would preserve sits inside a model already **1.529°** off truth on the same axis. **(B) is now closed, not deferred** — it would have carried old's internal contradiction forward and re-split the concept, and its only argument was old-pixel parity, which §2(a) subordinates.
   **Precondition (2) — fold-at-load vs per-frame: DISSOLVED under (A)** — the accumulated walk is already per-frame and already correct; no fold is needed. Returns only if (A) is ever reopened.
   **IMPLEMENTED + PREDICTIVELY VERIFIED (2026-07-17, same-day follow-up session — §11.35)**: gate run first (orientation_check.py on live dumps: live render/observer contradiction measured 23.4422° pre-fix, **0.0000° post-fix**; fix ≡ old observer at 0.0000° incl. the Moon; Charon 115.60→0.0000 by commutation), then the consolidation landed (single-authority walk in ModularBody, §11.35), then P1–P5 green on all five scenes (P2 ≤ 8.3e-8 every fresh body, Moon included, under the accumulated model). **One claim of this entry was REFUTED by the gate and is corrected in §11.35**: "divergence set closed at 7 bodies / all other moons provably unaffected" was FALSE — the exhaustive check grepped `rot_obliquity` keys and missed the **pole-RA/DE-derived rotation elements** both parse paths compute (the very conversion at ModularSystem.cpp:430); every Mars/Jupiter/Saturn/Uranus/Neptune moon carries a non-identity parent factor. Corrected spectrum in §11.35; the (A) direction survives its changed precondition on its own reasons (self-consistency, physical truth, script coverage — each re-verified, none rested on the 7-body count).
7. **`deduceBodyModuleList` coverage** — **RESOLVED** [vixy, plan phase 2026-07-11]: deduction extended per ported family (existing .ini files keep working) PLUS explicit module/slot declaration params for overrides and customs; param syntax lands with the first multi-module body (D2). Contract documented on `loadModule`.
9. **Executor-mode dissolution — DIRECTION RESOLVED [vixy: 2026-07-17, on the §11.36 report], implementation open.** The modes (inSolarSystem/inGalaxy/inUniverse) are **not kept by the new path — they must be emergent through the system nesting** (G2 chain membership), *"unified and still doing what they were doing as efficiently as they did"*. This is the stated intent behind EnvironmentModule's existence — *"part of the reason why EnvironmentModule exists, although not limited to it"* — i.e. the environment members carry the camera-location-relative slice, and the rest of the mode content becomes tree content. **Tully shall be handled as well** [vixy, explicit].
   - **Mode-content inventory (the migration surface; observed in the three executor modules)**: solar mode: `oort` (altitude-gated draw, solarSystemModule:154/196) · inGalaxy: `milky_way` 3D regime, `dso3d`, `starLines`, `starNav`, `dsoNav`, `cloudNav`, `ojmMgr(IN_GALAXY)` · inUniverse: `tully`, `starGalaxy`, `dsoNav`, `ojmMgr(IN_UNIVERSE)` · mode-independent overlays riding every mode (VR360, skyDisplayMgr person) — presumably stay frame-level, not tree content. `OjmMgr::STATE_POSITION` is itself a mode-keyed selector — same dissolution applies (OJM content attached at its floor).
   - **The mapping rule [vixy]**: mode content becomes **ModularBody instances at their floor level** — oort at/around the SolarSystem node (*"the oort and maybe some other might be harder to implement, though, yet they shall be implemented as modular body ideally, at different floor level maybe"*), dso3d/3D-milkyway-class content at the MilkyWay node, tully at the Universe node — with camera-location-relative rendering as EnvironmentModule members (the milkyway 2D/3D switch is already designed as exactly that, §3.8).
   - **Efficiency parity is a requirement, not a hope**: the old mode dispatch was also a work gate (only the active mode's content updates/draws). The nesting must reproduce it structurally: G4 gating applied at system-node level — content attached at a floor updates/draws only when its floor is in the active chain / visibly resolved. The §11.36 skeleton already carries the shape (drawSystemBodies gates nested systems at 16px; chain membership gates environments).
   - **What §11.36 already provides**: update-half mode independence (updateExperimental from Executor::update — the camera/chain lives in every mode); chain transitions Earth↔Universe verified both directions; the nested-draw dispatch + star proxy (skeleton, runtime-unexercised). ~~**What remains**: draw-half mode independence (`ssystemFactory->draw` is still called only by the solar/stellar modules — the draw twin of the §11.36 update defect);~~ **draw-half DONE 2026-07-23 (§11.80, code `14931a18`): `drawExperimental()` called from the inGalaxy/inUniverse modules, drawNested/drawStarProxy live + verified (0-vs-nonzero counterfactual).** What remains: the content migration itself (point-cloud/instanced module families for oort/dso3d/tully — the row-15 VOLUMETRIC slot and the MINOR_BODY/cluster traits are the reserved carriers, choice open); ojmMgr state dissolution; per-floor efficiency verification; the old-content/new-dot double-representation observed live (starNav's Sol vs the proxy dot, §11.80) resolves with the content migration.
   - **Horizon note vs §11.33's spec-8 gap list**: tully/dsoNavigator/etc. must render correctly under all projection modes NOW (§11.33 resolution stands — they live until absorbed), AND their content migrates into the unified path LATER (this entry). Two horizons, both real; neither cancels the other.

4. **Module wave 1** (line/2D, high script visibility): ORBIT, TRAIL, AXIS, GRID, STAR — each port stress-tests the loader contract before it ossifies.
5. **Module wave 2** (resource/shadow-coupled): RING (+G7 shadow service families), ATMOSPHERE rim, TAIL, OJM, MESH layered variants (Earth close-up parity).
6. **S4 — G5 handoff implementation** (parallel track, Vixy-paced: EntityCore Taskable authority): frame-task/cadencer restructure + ResourceHub; then the D4 surface-segment module as proving client; iterative updating (§3.2) rides the same restructure.
7. **S6/S7/S8 sweep**: seam completion (zoom fov, zoom_offset, sky-lock, hidden-body decision, texture variants, body_trace relocation), CameraAnchors, BodyDecor aggregation; then one exhaustive AppCommandInterface command-surface audit against §9.
8. **Switchover**: default-new, old-path update off (A/B kept as harness mode), removal Vixy-gated.

## 8. Threading / handoff strategy — PROPOSAL v2 (2026-07-11, pending Vixy convergence)

Successor to the strategy.txt task-at-draw sketch (confirmed best-found-at-the-time [vixy: 2026-07-11]). v1 proposed a single global completion queue; the EntityCore/Executor inventory (§8.6) found that Vixy's in-progress `Taskable` primitive [observed: EntityCore/Executor/Taskable.hpp, written 2026-07-11] provides strict per-body ordering [vixy: 2026-07-11 — named requirement] and a *better* resolution of the MPSC transient-window than v1's defer-retry. v2 is the synthesis. Once converged and implemented, the authoritative home of each contract becomes the header of the component owning it; this section then reduces to a pointer.

### 8.1 Constraints it must satisfy
C1 no loading↔drawing race · C2 no body destruction with pending tasks · C3 draw thread never blocks · C4 loader threads may block · strict per-body task ordering · burst absorption without idle-case cost (D7) · shared-resource dedup + fan-out (D7) · minimal overhead · D5 hardware ranges · D6 texture/model asymmetry (all [vixy: 2026-07-11]).

### 8.2 Shape: per-body Taskable chains + global ready-list, boundary-localized lock-freedom
Lock-free machinery exists **only** where the render thread meets producers; loader-side internals may block (C4 — INT-2: complexity lives where the binding boundary is).

1. **The render chain is an "effective thread"** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: draw-visible state is protected by one render-side `Taskable`; everything touching that state runs as a Task on its chain. Sequential consistency comes from the Taskable ordering promise, **not from thread identity** — rendering itself may occasionally execute on a worker or events thread, and that is correct by design. C1 = chain serialization; per-body publish ordering is automatic (a single FIFO chain preserves per-key order). The chain **may and should go idle** — see the stack note in 4.
2. **Main loop** [vixy: 2026-07-11, corrected 2nd pass]: a pure cadencer at the target framerate — `execute()`s the **frame task** onto the render chain (inplace when idle, lowest latency; chained behind pending publishes otherwise) and waits until the next tick. **Update+draw live inside the frame task itself** — one Task, scheduled/executed every frame [vixy]: the residual synchronous update part is cheap, cache-local, and uses the exact elapsed time in non-recording mode.
3. **Two protection domains** `[inferred — effectively confirmed by the fence discipline below]`: work tasks/chains do the hard work and the async sequencing (completion events: the chain waits, no thread waits) and **never touch draw-visible state**; their final step schedules a lightweight subscribe/finalize/publish task onto the render chain [vixy: 2026-07-11 — "tasks on the Taskable for rendering only subscribe/finalize the render-thread-side part, not doing hard work"]. **Fence discipline** [vixy: 2026-07-11, 2nd pass]: fence waits happen as late as possible, only where the touched resource requires them, and **only the rendering part ever waits on a fence** — with multiple frames in flight, the acquire/present semaphore structure makes an actual block genuinely unlikely; the fence is the guarantee, not the expected wait. The separation restated with this: long holds belong to the work domain; the render chain carries only late, rarely-blocking guarantees. Starvation-free: publish execution is independent of whether the body is drawn (closes the D3 channel of the at-draw sketch).
4. **Cost profile**: idle = zero — no drain point exists; the render chain sleeps between frame tasks. Per completion = the work chain's exchanges + one publish push. Bursts chain behind the frame task and execute between frames as O(N) lightweight publishes (heavy work — decode, build, upload — already done in the work domain; a publish is a pointer/descriptor swap, O(small) by contract). **Stack note** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: synchronous `endTask` cascades nest on the stack (unless tail-call optimization applies), so chains going idle is *preferable* — it resets the depth; bounded bursts (D7) are the sizing assumption.
5. **Lifetime (C2) under the granularity decision** [vixy: 2026-07-11, 2nd pass]: synchronization goes through a **single Taskable** — the render chain — likely sufficient granularity, and cheaply revisable if it turns out wrong, thanks to how Task/Taskable compose [vixy]. **All Taskables live for the whole application duration** → `TASKABLE_REFERENCE_COUNT`/`TASKABLE_TASK_KEEP_REFERENCE` stay off. Two consequences:
   - **Pins need no atomics at all**: pin increments (request emission, inside the frame task) and decrements (publish/finalization tasks) both execute within the effective thread — a plain integer under chain serialization.
   - **The hand-off rule stops being discipline and becomes forced**: with a non-atomic pin, worker code *cannot* legally release — the only path is transferring the hold into the publish task. The earlier `[inferred]` rule is now enforced by the type, not by convention.
   `remove()` on a pinned body detaches from the tree immediately (world-consistent, ModularBodyPtr::redirect philosophy); memory follows when the pin reaches zero inside the chain. Reopening trigger (membership, not magnitude): sustained publish volume contending with frame-task latency → split to per-body chains — KEEP_REFERENCE returns, and §8.6(f) must be fixed first.
   - Pin counter deliberately **not** merged with render-local `pointerCount` (different synchronization domains; merging forces atomics onto the render-local path — I2: one info, one domain).
   - Module replacement during pending load needs no pin: integration validates `body->slot(id) == module` at drain — render-thread-local, race-free by construction, drops stale payloads.
6. **Requests, priorities, cancellation**: `AsyncBuilder`-style atomic priority field is the existing pattern ("prioritize on activation" = one atomic store); workers re-read at pop and re-check before expensive phases (drops uploads for bodies gone halo-only). Cancelled tasks still schedule a payload-less completion for the unpin. The `LoadPriority` fused state+priority encoding (one atomic carries DONE/COMPLETED/LOADING/LAZY→NOW) is kept — one-word lifecycle — but needs a header comment and the NOW→ACTIVE rename for consistency.
7. **Shared resources (D7)**: the loadable unit is a refcounted resource object (`AsyncBuilder::useCount` pattern exists). Dedup by resource identity at request time (render-side registry; s_texture already dedups by name — verify overlap at plan time). On completion, fan-out = schedule one integration task per attached (body, module) — each push lock-free, per-body order preserved. Attachment-list synchronization: either a small loader-side mutex (legal, C4) or the resource itself as a `Taskable` in `execute` mode (attachments serialized as tasks, no lock) — plan-mode choice.
8. **Texture residency (D6)**: `gc.hpp` is the existing eviction machinery — cycle-based grace (`preservationCycles`) is the hysteresis; `used()` at draw is the relevance mark. Eviction decisions stay render-side; VkImage destruction deferred by frames-in-flight. VRAM budget = D5 parameter with degradation ladder. Models: same pipeline, no eviction path (D6). `gc` cross-thread sync has a known open point (`deletable` non-atomic, flagged in-source) — verify at plan time.
9. **Task semantics — the contract** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: strict ordering applies **only inside `[start(), endTask())`**; a task's `start()` returning does *not* mean the task is done — `endTask` being called on it does; a task must hold nothing with side effects past its `endTask`; outside the window, no guarantee. Tasks/Taskables compose (a Task may hold another Task which calls the enclosing task's `endTask`).
   → **Asynchronous chain holds**: a task may start async work (GPU transfer, fence) and call `endTask` later from wherever completion fires — the *chain* waits, no *thread* waits. This is the natural carrier for the upload→integrate sequence with ordering preserved.
   → ~~Render-hop pattern~~ **SUPERSEDED same day** by the effective-thread model (§8.2.1/3): no thread affinity exists to restore — cross-thread continuation is correct because protection is per-chain, not per-thread; the "hop" is simply the work chain's natural final step of publishing onto the render chain. Kept as a record of the wrong frame (affinity-thinking) it came from.
10. **Thread model** [vixy: 2026-07-11, extended 2nd pass] (concretizes the draft inventory at ModularBody.hpp:131-137): 1 video-player thread (video frame loading) · 1 main thread as cadencer (frame-task `execute` at target framerate, opportunistic inplace execution) · 1 events/scripts thread (SDL event polling, scripts, non-recurrent actions: body building — heavy part lazy — with attachment published as a render-chain Task, orbits/hints display, detach/remove, move, selection) · 1 compute thread (shadow projection, owns a compute VkQueue) [initially forgotten — vixy] · 1 worker executing **only ACTIVE-priority** tasks (a guaranteed free lane for the "needed now" class — reactivity) · nproc−3 generic workers (default, configurable) executing all tasks. The dedicated threads are rarely stressed simultaneously (< 3 active at a time in real-world use) and don't compete with other programs → REALTIME FIFO scheduling shouldn't hurt latency by design [vixy]; the "< 3 active" property is the load-bearing claim, not the exact thread count. **Open integration item** [vixy, initially forgotten]: screen capture for video rendering must fit the task system — `[inferred sketch]` readback rides the render chain after the frame task, encode is worker-domain, file write on the capture path's own ordering.

### 8.3 Rejected alternatives (with rejecting precondition)
- Per-body mutex: C3 + D5-floor (preempted holder stalls render a scheduling quantum); unconditional hot-path cost.
- Task execution at the owning body's draw (strategy.txt sketch): D3 starvation — halo-only bodies never draw → tasks never run → pin/VRAM leak. Its ordering property is retained via Taskable; its execution trigger is replaced by the ready-list drain.
- v1 single global completion queue (2026-07-11, same day): no per-body ordering guarantee across producers (two loaders finishing LoD1/LoD2 of one body can interleave); transient-window handled by defer-retry (one frame latency) where Taskable's marker-exchange resolves it with zero latency and exactly-once continuation. Superseded by §8.2.
- v2.1 never-idle render chain (2026-07-11, same day): a self-rescheduling frame task keeping the chain permanently busy bought "affinity by construction" — at the price of **unbounded stack growth**: synchronous `endTask` cascades nest, and a chain that never ends never resets the depth (unless TCO) [vixy: 2026-07-11]. The stack was the uncounted resource in the "by construction" reasoning. Superseded by the effective-thread model, where idle chains are correct (inplace execution is serialized by the chain promise, no affinity needed) and going idle resets the stack.
- Per-module atomic payload slots: O(visible) per-frame checks for rare events; check cost dominates event cost.
- shared_ptr lifetime: G8. · Generation counters + indirection: same guarantee as pins, more machinery. · Blocking flush on removal: blocks whichever thread removes.

### 8.4 Preconditions (design invalid if violated)
1. **All draw-visible state mutation happens through Tasks on the render chain** — reformulated 2026-07-11 from "on the render thread": the effective-thread model makes physical thread identity irrelevant; what must hold is that no draw-visible state is touched outside the chain. Scripts/events build offline and publish via chain Tasks (e.g. body attachment) — **VERIFIED as the intended management path** [vixy: 2026-07-11].
2. Integration tasks are O(small); violations show as frame spikes at drain (measurable, attributable).
3. Loaders read bodies only via pinned references; mutable inputs are snapshotted into the request at emission (render-side).

### 8.5 Open parameters (plan-mode decisions, not architecture)
Pool sizing policy over 4–64 cores · VRAM budget detection + degradation ladder values · `preservationCycles`/hysteresis values · attachment-list mechanism (mutex vs resource-as-Taskable) · overlap between s_texture dedup and the resource registry · **Task memory-validity flag candidate** (per §8.6.d "maybe worth a new flag"): dual-hold count on Task — init 2 (execution + linkage); `endTask` releases the execution hold (both on CAS-success, since no successor can then exist); the successor's producer releases the linkage hold after its `next.exchange`; at 0 → `recycle()`. Under ifdef, zero overhead when the external guarantee (task embedded in refcounted resource object) is used instead.

### 8.6 EntityCore/Executor inventory (2026-07-11) — reuse map + draft defects
**Reusable as-is or with port**: `AsyncLoaderMgr` (worker pools, BigSave native cache, O_DIRECT once-reads, priority scan, minPriority gate) — its main-thread `update()` pump (O(all-pending) scan per frame, poll not push — I3) is what the ready-list drain replaces; `AsyncBuilder` (refcount + atomic priority + two-phase asyncLoad/postLoad = the resource object pattern); `gc` (eviction/grace); `Taskable`/`Task` (the ordering primitive, in-progress).
**Defects found in the 2026-07-11 `Taskable.hpp` draft** (statuses per Vixy review, 2026-07-11):
- (a) Local shadows member: **RESOLVED on disk** — `execute()` renamed by Vixy; `scheduleExecution()` applied by Claude (2026-07-11, authorized). GCC behavior clarified [vixy]: the new local wins (consistent with the standard point-of-declaration reading; earlier divergence note was a misparse on my side — dissolved).
- (b) Missing memory edge on the marker handoff: **RESOLVED on disk** — producer half (`acq_rel` lines 21/32) by Vixy; consumer half (`endTask` marker exchange → `acq_rel`) applied by Claude (2026-07-11, authorized).
- (c) `Task::start` undefined → link error: **FIXED by Vixy** (now pure virtual; verified on disk 2026-07-11).
- **Contract comments written** (2026-07-11, per Vixy's "specify the promises where they weren't made explicit"): Taskable.hpp carries the full promise block (effective-thread framing, ordering window, completion = endTask, no-affinity, memory validity/marker window, scheduleExecution == false meaning, stack-nesting caution); Task.hpp carries the completion-semantics pointer.
- (d) Node reclamation: **RESOLVED as contract, promises to be written into the headers** [vixy: 2026-07-11 — "definitely worth specifying the promises where they weren't made explicit"]: ordering guarantee only within `[start, endTask)`; task holds nothing with side effects past `endTask`; lifetime via `TASKABLE_TASK_KEEP_REFERENCE` or an external guarantee (ifdef = zero overhead when external); not pooling. Memory residual **confirmed as real and hard to determine externally** [vixy] — a Task's storage must stay valid until successor-linked or chain-empty (the successor's producer may write `task->next` after `endTask` returned); "maybe worth a new flag" [vixy] → candidate design in §8.5.
- (e) `delete this` on releasing thread: **RESOLVED BY STRUCTURE** [vixy: 2026-07-11] — see §8.2.5: lightweight render-chain finalization tasks carry the releases, so the last release lands on the render thread by construction; the checkable hand-off rule is `[inferred — pending convergence]`.
- (f) `TASKABLE_TASK_KEEP_REFERENCE` acquire/release asymmetry (found 2026-07-11, second pass): `execute()`'s empty-chain branch acquires but `scheduleExecution()`'s does not, while `endTask`'s CAS-success releases unconditionally → scheduled-mode chain start with both ifdefs on underflows the refcount → premature delete. **MOOT under the chosen granularity** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: single-chain synchronization, all Taskables application-lifetime, flags off. Remains **latent** — must be fixed before any per-body-chain split enables the flag (reopening trigger in §8.2.5).
**Defects in existing `AsyncLoaderMgr` (pre-dating, lower priority — the port supersedes them)**: `addLoad`/`addBuild` `push_front` without holding the list spinlock while workers iterate under it (list-head data race); `AsyncLoader::priority` is plain (non-atomic) but written by worker and main threads (`AsyncBuilder::priority` is atomic — the asymmetry looks unintended); `update()`'s lock-busy branch iterates the list without the lock while a worker may scan it.

## 9. SSystemFactory seam table (Phase C, 2026-07-11)

The external world reaches the body system exclusively through `SSystemFactory` (callers: coreLink.cpp, core.cpp, executorModule). Per category, where each call routes when the new path is active (`drawModularSystem`). "dual" = already routed to both paths today.

| Category | Old-surface examples | New-path route |
|---|---|---|
| Selection/search | setSelected, searchByNamesI18, searchAround, listMatchingObjectsI18n | `ModularBody::findBody*` + `ModularBodySelector` + `ModularObject` (B7 surface); pointer draw via ObjectBase::drawPointer over ModularObject (D2) |
| Queries | getSelectedAZ/ALT/RA/DE, getPlanetsPosition, getSunAltitude/Azimuth | `ModularObject::getAltAz/getRaDeValue` (Camera-conversion idiom); az convention verify at D2 |
| Scale/size | setScale, setPlanetSizeScale, set/getMoonScale, setSizeLimit | `ModularBody::setScaling` (ASmooth) + global params; moon/sun scale = hardcoded-body scaling; **setPlanetSizeScale now DUAL (T7 §11.45 — `planet_scale` was the §11.35 seam gap, now `findBody→setScaling`)** |
| Color | setBodyColor/getBodyColor, setDefaultBodyColor | **RUNTIME SETTERS DUAL (2026-07-22, §11.65, B29)**: per-body recolor (halo=body, label/orbit/trail=modules, self-select I4) + `"all"` broadcast + runtime DEFAULT (module statics) mirror to the new path; per-instance storage + broadcast override (§11.48(b)); getter old-authority pre-switchover (§11.46). Runtime colour PERSISTENCE across `body action reload` = B16 §11.55(i)'s suspended body-scoped-override decision, NOT closed here (old has no reload ⇒ no observable to reproduce; new rebuilds-from-file resets, measured) |
| Flags | setFlagHints/Axis/Trails/Orbits/Clouds, setPlanetHidden, toggleHideSatellites | module static `show` flags (HintModule etc.) + `ModularBody::hide/show`; per-name orbit flag = per-body module toggle |
| Textures | switchPlanetTexMap, createTexSkin, planetTesselation | **ALL DUAL (2026-07-19, §11.46)**: skin pair = `BodyModule::createTexSkin/switchTexSkin` broadcast (MESH modules consume, old tex_current parity) + factory mirrors; tesselation was ALREADY dual by shared `BodyTesselation` object (row 2 §11.26 — the T7 audit row claiming OLD-ONLY was wrong, correction at §11.45) |
| Body add/remove | addBody, preloadBody, removeBody, removeSupplementalBodies | `ModularSystem::loadBody` (dual for removeBody already); preload = `ModularBody::preload`. **New-path-only addition (2026-07-21, §11.55): `body action reload` → `SSystemFactory::reloadCurrentSystem` → `ModularSystem::reloadSystem`** — rebuild the current system from its data file, observer state kept; no old-path counterpart |
| Anchors/camera | cameraMoveToPoint/Body, transitionTo*, alignCameraToBody, saveCameraPosition, switchToAnchor | `Camera` (motion/reference layer, live) + `CameraAnchors` sketch (named/persistence/scripted layer — second pass); **bookmark fov (position save/load) now DUAL (T7 §11.45 — CoreBackup::loadBackup mirrors Camera::setHalfFov)**; ~~view_offset/zoom_offset still OLD-ONLY — B17 CHARACTERISED + SUSPENDED (§11.63): a Navigator/VIEW fov-coupled rotation (not a projector offset), the new path has no projection-stage hook; projection-space-vs-parity is Vixy's~~ **view_offset now DUAL (B17 LANDED 2026-07-24, §11.92 — D6 option (1), render-only Camera pitch; line de-staled §11.102(f); two enumeration residuals §11.102(b): fifth arm site + query-channel skew)** |
| Per-frame | computePositions, computePreDraw, draw, update | frame task on the render chain (D1): update+draw fused; computePreDraw's buckets → Renderer depth partitioning (D4) |
| System transitions | enterSystem/leaveSystem, changeSystem | `Camera::switchToBody`/`warpToBody` + `ModularSystem::systemOf` |
| Tools | bodyTrace pen (upPen/downPen/togglePen) | stays a coreModule-level tool (not a body-render concern) |
| Fonts | registerFont | `HintModule::setFont` at the seam |
